THIS WRIT PETITION IS FILED U/A 226 & 227 OF
CONSTITUTION OF INDIA PRAYING TO DIRECT THE
RESPONDENTS NOT TO TERMINATE THE SERVICES OF THE
PETITIONERS & CONTINUE THE SERVICES OF THE
PETITIONERS TILL THE CASE OF THE PETITIONES IS
CONSIDERED AND ETC.

378
THESE PETITIONS HAVING BEEN HEARD AND
RESERVED FOR ORDERS, THIS DAY, H.N.NAGAMOHAN DAS.
J, PRONOUNCED THE FOLLOWING;
O R D E R
In these writ petitions different prayers are made. The sum and
substance of the prayers made in all these writ petitions is as under:
i) to direct the respondents to extend the benefit of
Karnataka Residential Educational Institutions
(Absorption of Persons working as Principals and
Teachers in Morarji Desai and Kittur Rani Chennamma
residential schools on contract basis into establishment of
Karnataka Residential Educational Institutions Society)
(Special Regulations), 2011” (for short ‘Absorption
Regulations, 2011’);
ii) to quash the Karnataka Residential Education Society
(Cadre and Recruitment) Regulations, 2011 (for short
‘Recruitment Regulations, 2011’) in so far as it relates to
granting service weightage only to the principals and

379
teachers appointed by the Karnataka Residential
Educational Institutions Society (for short ‘Society’);
iii) to direct the respondents to extend the benefit of service
weightage as specified under the Recruitment
Regulations, 2011 to the petitioners;
iv) to quash the recruitment notification dated 27.04.2011
inviting applications to fill the vacancies of principals
and teachers; and
v) to direct the respondents to continue the services of
petitioners in the residential schools where they are now
working and for other reliefs.
2. Till 1977-78, the private educational institutions and the
religious mutts were running the residential schools in the State of
Karnataka. In the year 1977 the Social Welfare Department started
residential schools to the children of tribal community under the name
of “Ashrama Schools”. In these schools the tribal children in the age
group of 5 to 10 years were provided with free education, boarding

380
and lodging. Subsequently in the year 1986 the Government of India
started residential schools in every District in the country under the
name of “Navodaya Model Residential schools” with an object to
encourage the talented and meritorious children belonging to
Scheduled Castes, Scheduled Tribes, Minorities and other Backward
Classes residing in rural areas. Thereafter in the year 1994-95 the
Government of Karnataka started residential schools in the rural areas
to encourage the talented and meritorious children by providing free
and quality education. These residential schools were imparting
education to the girls and boys under different names such as (i)
Navodaya Model Residential Schools (ii) Morarji Desai Model
Residential Schools (iii) Ekalavya Residential Schools (iv) Kittoor
Rani Chennamma Residential Schools and (v) Minority Residential
schools. These residential schools were under the control of Social
Welfare Department, Backward Class Department, Tribal Welfare
Department and Education Department. In these residential schools
free education, boarding, lodging, medical facility, uniform, books,
study material, furniture and computers were provided. There was no

381
cadre and recruitment rules to these different residential schools in the
matter of recruitment of teaching and non-teaching staff. From time
to time the Government issued orders/notifications/guidelines for
appointment of teaching and non-teaching staff temporarily on
contract basis. Accordingly, the teaching and non-teaching staff were
appointed in these residential schools temporarily on contract basis.
3. In order to bring these residential schools under the
control, supervision and management of one authority, the
Government of Karnataka on 6.10.1999 registered a society called as
“Karnataka Residential Educational Institutions Society” (for short
‘Society’) under the provisions of Karnataka Societies Registration
Act, 1960. All these residential schools across the State were brought
under this Society. Further the bye-laws of the society provides for
not only managing the existing schools but also to start new schools.
Accordingly, the society started new residential schools in addition to
the existing residential schools. Even in the schools started by the

382
society the teaching and non-teaching staff were appointed
temporarily on contract basis.
4. From the Academic year 2004-2005, the administration and
management of these residential schools was handed over to Zilla
Panchayaths in the respective Districts by order dated 5.10.2004.
Subsequently in the year 2006, the Government of Karnataka issued a
manual for residential schools specifying the procedure and guidelines
for day to day management, supervision and control of these
residential schools. Further the society requested the respective
Deputy Commissioners, Zilla Panchayaths, Social Welfare
Department and Backward class department to appoint teaching and
non-teaching staff temporarily on contract basis to the residential
schools. Accordingly, the Deputy Commissioners and Zilla
panchayaths appointed teaching and non-teaching staff through an
Agency in these residential schools. As of now there are 542
residential schools and colleges in different parts of Karnataka.

383
5. The rules and regulations of the society provides to make
regulations governing the service conditions of the employees of the
society. Accordingly, the ‘Recruitment Regulations, 2011’ came into
force w.e.f. 21.1.2011. These Recruitment Regulations, 2011
specifies the category of posts, method of recruitment, minimum
qualification, promotion, absorption, retirement, service weightage
etc. For the purpose of these writ petitions the regulation relating to
service weightage is important. It is specified that while preparing the
merit list, weightage of 5 marks for each completed year of service
shall be given subject to a maximum of 40 marks adding to the total
percentage of qualifying examination marks in respect of candidates
appointed by Karnataka Residential Education Institution Society on
contract basis as one time measure.
6. When the matter stood at that stage, the contract employees
working in these residential schools requested the Government of
Karnataka for regularisation of their services. After due consideration
the Government of Karnataka enacted ‘Absorption Regulations,

384
2011’. These Absorption Regulations provides for absorption of
principals and teachers appointed prior to the academic year 2004-
2005 and continued till the date of commencement of the regulations
as one time measure subject to satisfaction of certain other conditions.
Accordingly, the respondents have absorbed 56 principals and 436
teachers.
7. The Recruitment Regulations, 2011 provides for service
weightage to the contract teachers appointed by the society only. But
these Regulations do not provide the benefit of service weightage to
the contract teachers appointed through agency. The petitioners who
are appointed as teachers through agency are before this Court calling
in question the Recruitment Regulations 2011 insofar as it relates to
extending the service weightage only to the teachers appointed by the
society and for a writ of mandamus directing the respondents to
extend the same benefit to the petitioners who are appointed through
agency. The petitioners are similarly situated to that of Principals and
teachers appointed prior to 2004-2005, but they are not absorbed. The

385
petitioners contend that they are entitled for absorption under
Absorption Regulations, 2011. Without extending the benefit of
absorption and service weightage, the respondents have issued
recruitment notification dated 27.4.2011 to appoint Principals and
teachers in place of petitioners and as such the same is to be quashed.
Hence, these writ petitions.
8. Sri Raghavendra G.Gayathri and Smt.Manjula R.Kamadalli,
learned Government Pleaders are directed to take notice for
respondent – State of Karnataka and the authorities working under it.
Sri M.N.Madhusudhan, learned standing counsel is directed to take
notice for the respondent – Society. Notice to the Zilla Panchayaths is
hereby dispensed. The respondent – State Government has filed
statement of objections. The respondent – Society filed objections
and additional objections. By consent of learned Advocates on both
the side the writ petitions are heard on merits.

386
9. Learned advocates for the petitioners contend that they are
appointed at the instance of society and as per the conditions imposed
by the society. The agency appointed the petitioners by notifying in
the press, following the merit and roaster system. Further it is
contended that extending the benefit of service weightage to the
contract teachers appointed by the society and denying the same to the
petitioners who are appointed at the instance of the society is nothing
but discrimination and violative of Articles 14, 16 and 21 of the
Constitution. Petitioners are working from 2004-05 and 2009-2010
and at this length of time denying the benefit of service weightage is
arbitrary, illegal and contrary to law. Absorption of Principals and
teachers appointed by the Society prior to 2004-2005 and not
absorbing the petitioners who are appointed subsequent to 2004-2005
is nothing but discrimination. Petitioners are similarly placed with the
principals and teachers appointed by the Society. Therefore, the
petitioners are entitled for absorption. Reliance is placed on few
decisions.

387
10. Learned counsel for the respondents contend that in the
Recruitment Regulations 2011 service weightage is provided only to
the contract teachers appointed by the society. Subsequently the
Absorption Regulations 2011 was enacted and contract teachers
appointed by the society prior to 2004-05 and continued in service as
on the date of Absorption Regulations are all absorbed. The provision
relating to service weightage is technically continuing in the
Recruitment Regulation, 2011. The petitioners who are all appointed
through agency are not entitled for any service weightage. There is no
discrimination and violation of any of the Articles of the Constitution.
Reliance is placed on number of decisions.
11. Heard arguments on both the side and perused the entire
writ papers. Though several grounds are taken in the memorandum of
writ petitions finally the following are the only points that are
principally canvassed:
i) Whether the petitioners are entitled for absorption under
the provisions of Absorption Regulations, 2011?

388
ii) Whether the petitioners are entitled for service weightage
as specified in the Recruitment Regulations, 2011?
iii) Whether the recruitment notification dated 27.4.2011 is
liable to be quashed?
On point No.(i)
12. Learned Advocates for the petitioners have not advanced
any arguments in so far as it relates to validity of Recruitment
Regulations, 2011 and the Absorption Regulations, 2011. Therefore,
it is not necessary for this court to go into the constitutional validity of
these regulations. Learned counsel for the petitioners contend that the
principals and teachers appointed by the Society prior to 2004-2005
and continued in service till the date of coming into force of
Absorption Regulations, 2011 are entitled for absorption. This benefit
of absorption is denied to the petitioners firstly on the ground that they
are not appointed by the Society and secondly on the ground that they
are appointed subsequent to 2004-2005 by Agency. The material on
record discloses that prior to 2000, the principals and teachers were

389
appointed by Social Welfare Department, Backward class Department
and the Education Department. After the formation of the Society the
residential schools were brought under its control from the year 2000.
The principals and teachers who were appointed prior to 2000 are
continued under the society. Subsequent to 2000, the Society
appointed principals and teachers up to the year 2004-2005 and they
are continued in service as on the date of Absorption Regulations,
2011 came into force. The respondents by issuing notifications,
holding selection process and by following reservation and roster
appointed the principals and teachers. These teachers have put in
service of 7 to 10 years and more. Having regard to the age of these
principals and teachers, length of service rendered by them and their
appointment on merit and by following the reservation and roster,
constitute a class by themselves. In the circumstances, the
Government after active consideration passed the impugned
Absorption Regulations, 2011 only to such of the principals and
teachers who are appointed prior to 2004-2005 and continued in
service upto the date of impugned regulations came into force in 2011

390
subject to satisfying certain other conditions. Therefore, the
principals and teachers appointed prior to 2004-2005 constitute a
separate class.
13. On the other hand the petitioners are all appointed
subsequent to 2004-2005 through an Agency. Though some of these
petitioners are appointed pursuant to notifications, holding of
selection process and following reservation, constitute by themselves
a different class. Further the petitioners are all appointed through
Agency and their salaries are paid through agency. Having regard to
the nature of appointment, length of service and their age, constitute
by themselves a different class. The Government by taking into
consideration these relevant factors not extended the benefit of
absorption to the principals and teachers appointed subsequent to
2004-2005.
14. The Supreme Court in Shekawath Ali vs. State of
Orissa (AIR 1955 SC 166) held that legislation enacted for the

391
achievement of a particular job or purpose need not be of
embracing. It is for the Legislature to determine what categories it
would embrace within the scope of legislation. Merely because
certain categories which would stand on the same footing as those
which are covered by the legislation are left out would not render
legislation which has been enacted in any manner discriminatory
and violative of fundamental rights guaranteed by Article 14.
Therefore the denial of benefit of absorption to the petitioners will not
amount to discrimination, arbitrary and illegal. Therefore point no.(i)
is answered in negative.
On Point No.(ii)
15. As already stated the principals and teachers appointed
prior to 2004-2005 and continued in service till 2011 are all covered
under Absorption Regulations, 2011. Further the government by its
notification dated 24.04.2011 has already absorbed 56 principals and
380 teachers under the Absorption Regulations, 2011. The grievance
of the petitioners is that in the Recruitment Regulations, 2011 the

392
service weightage is provided only to the principals and teachers
appointed by the society. The Society contends that they have not
appointed any principals and teachers subsequent to 2004-2005. Then
for whose benefit the service weightage is provided under the
Recruitment Regulations, 2011? Therefore, the very object of
providing service weightage in the Recruitment Regulations, 2011 is
only for the benefit of the temporary contract principals and teachers
appointed subsequent to 2004-2005 and continued in service. In the
circumstances of these cases this is the inevitable conclusion that is to
be drawn stating that the service weightage is for the benefit of
petitioners. Therefore, this benefit of service weightage cannot be
denied to the petitioners.
16. The contention of learned counsel for the respondents that
when Recruitment Regulations, 2011 was enacted they had no idea of
enactment of Absorption Regulations, 2011. Therefore, the intention
of providing service weightage in the Recruitment Regulations, 2011
was only to the principals and teachers appointed by the society prior

393
to 2004-2005 is unacceptable to me. It is not in dispute that prior to
2004-2005 the society appointed principals and teachers and so also
the Social Welfare Department, Backward classes Department and
Education Department. The Recruitment Regulations, 2011 do not
specify service weightage to the principals and teachers appointed
prior to 2000 by the Social Welfare Department, Backward classes
Department and Education Department. Therefore, there is no
substance in the contention of learned counsel for the respondents that
the provision relating to service weightage is technically continued in
the Recruitment Regulations, 2011 and that the same is not meant for
petitioners who are not appointed by the society.
17. Admittedly, from 2004-2005 onwards these residential
schools are under the control of Zilla Panchayaths in the respective
Districts. The Government of Karnataka in their statement of
objections filed before this court specifically stated that to fill up the
vacancies in the newly started schools subsequent to 2004-2005 the
Society requested the respective Deputy Commissioners, Zilla

394
Panchayhaths, Social Welfare Department, Backward Class
Departments to engage services of contract teachers by outsourcing on
temporary basis. Accordingly, the petitioners are all appointed at the
instance of third respondent society. The appointment of the
petitioners through agency is again as per the manual of residential
schools issued by the Government of Karnataka through Social
Welfare Department, the Standard tendered document issued by the
Government of Karnataka and also the orders issued by the Society.
In some of the Districts, the selection of Principals and teachers was
made through Nehru Yuva Kendras and in some other Districts
directly by Zilla Panchayaths and in some other Districts through
Agency. All these appointments are at the instance of the Society and
as per the guidelines prescribed by it and the Government. Though
the monthly salaries are paid through Agency the same is in terms of
the Manual for residential schools and the Standard tendered
document. Therefore, the appointment of petitioners was at the
instance of the Society. In the circumstances, I decline to accept the
contention of the respondents that in the matter of appointment of the

395
petitioners through Agency the society has no role to play and there is
no relationship of employer and employee. I hold that petitioners are
appointed by the Society and on its instructions, through Agency. In
the circumstances, the petitioners are entitled for the benefit of service
weightage under the Recruitment Regulations, 2011.
18. Learned counsel for the petitioners relying on a
unreported judgment of the Supreme Court in Karnataka State
Primary School vs. State of Karnataka in W.P.No.240/1990 vide order
dated 10.07.1991 contend that in similar circumstances the court
provided weightage and age relaxation. The relevant para in this
order of the Supreme Court reads as under:
Having heard learned counsel, we are of the view
that a direction should be issued by the State of Karnataka
to the Zilla Parishads to carry on the process of selection
in the normal way for filling up the vacancies giving
opportunity to the ad hoc teachers like the petitioners to
appear for selection and those of them who are found
qualified should be absorbed along with others who are

396
not petitioners before us. For the purpose of scrutinising
the claim of the ad hoc teachers in regard to their
suitability or eligibility the age bar would not be raised
because they are already in service. a principle for giving
weightage on the basis of experience may also be indicated
by the State Government. It could be on the basis of year
so that those who have already served for one year may
have a particular weightage and those who have put in
more than a year could have a higher weightage on yearly
basis.
The Supreme Court in R.S.Joshi vs. Ajit Mills Limited and
another AIR 1977 SC 2279, held as under:
The true key to constitutional construction is to
view the equity of the statute and sense the social mission
of the law, language permitting, against the triune facets
of justice, highlighted in the preamble to the Paramount
Parchment, read with a specious signification of the
listed entries concerned. If this programme is fed into
the judicial cerebration with the presumption of
constitutionality super added, the result would tell
whether the measure is ultra vires or not. The doctrine

397
of ancillary and incidental powers is also embraced
within this scheme of interpretation.
Justice Benjamin N.Cardozo, former Chief Justice of US
Supreme Court in his Lecture – The Methods of History, Tradition
and Sociology, observed thus:
I do not mean of course, that Judges are commissioned to
set aside existing rules at pleasure in favour of any other
set of rules which they may hold to be expedient or wise. I
mean that when they are called upon to say how far
existing rules are to be extended or restricted, they must
let the welfare of society fix the path, its direction and its
distance.
19. Keeping in view the above observations, it is necessary
to examine the purpose of providing service weightage in the
Recruitment Regulations, 2011. The social mission and the welfare of
society shall guide our path, direction and distance. Having regard to
the age, length of service rendered by the petitioners and the purpose

398
of providing service weightage in the regulations is for the benefit of
the petitioners. Therefore, I hold that petitioners are entitled for
weightage provided under the Recruitment Regulations, 2011.
Accordingly I answer point no.2 in affirmative.
On point No.(iii)
20. Learned counsel for the petitioners contend that in the
impugned notification dated 27.4.2011, respondents notified to fill up
4071 vacancies. Subsequently, the respondents increased the number
of vacancies on 15.11.2011 and 30.11.2011. This increase in the
number of vacancies under the very same notification is contrary to
law declared by the Supreme Court in State of Orissa vs. Raj Kishore
Nanda (2010) 6 SCC 777. There is no dispute with regard to the law
laid down by the Apex Court. But in the instant case, petitioners
submitted their applications in response to the recruitment notification
and participated in the selection process. Therefore, the petitioners
are now estopped from contending that the impugned recruitment
notification is bad in law. Therefore, I decline to accept this

399
contention of learned counsel for the petitioners. Accordingly, this
point is answered in negative.
21. Pursuant to the recruitment notification dated 27.4.2011,
the respondents conducted combined competitive examination.
Respondents further contend that the petitioners in the combined
competitive examination have not secured the qualifying marks
required for selection. Now the respondents have published the final
selection list and the additional selection list. Admittedly, the service
weightage was not extended to the petitioners while processing the
final selection list and the additional selection list. Therefore, the
respondents have to reissue the final selection list by extending the
benefit of service weightage to the petitioners.
22. Some of the petitioners are non-teaching staff in the
respondent residential schools. The Recruitment Regulations 2011
provides for grant of service weightage for temporary contract
Principals and teachers only. Therefore the non-teaching staff are not

400
entitled for the benefit of service weightage. Further the Absorption
Regulation, 2011 is applicable only to temporary contract Principals
and teachers. These Absorption Regulations are not applicable to the
non-teaching staff. Therefore the petitions filed by the non-teaching
staff are hereby dismissed.
For the reasons stated above, the following:
ORDER
i) Writ petitions are partly allowed.
ii) The petitioners are not entitled for absorption under the
Absorption Regulations, 2011.
iii) The respondents shall regularise the Principals and
teachers appointed prior to 2004-2005 and continued in
service as on the date of Absorption Regulations, 2011
came into force subject to fulfilling the other conditions.
iv) It is declared that all the Principals and teachers including
the petitioners herein appointed on or after 2004-2005 are

401
entitled for the benefit of service weightage as specified
in Recruitment Regulations, 2011.
v) The respondents to complete the selection process
pursuant to the recruitment notification dated 27.4.2011
by granting the benefit of service weightage to the
petitioners and all other similarly situated Principals and
teachers.
vi) Since respondents have already processed the
applications and issued final selection list and additional
selection list, they have to redo the process of selection
by extending the benefit of service weightage to the
petitioners and similarly situated candidates.
vii) Till the completion of selection process as stated above
the present placement of petitioners and others who are
continuing in service shall not be disturbed.
viii) The prayer in the writ petitions in so far as it relates to
quashing of recruitment notification dated 27.4.2011 is
hereby rejected.

402
ix) The writ petitions filed by the non-teaching staff are
hereby dismissed.
 Sd/-
 JUDGE.
DKB/LRS.
